# How a run flows. A scenario TOML file (a kind, a seed and its parameters) goes into the engine, kshana { $version }, through the api::run_toml dispatch over { "87" } scenario kinds, deterministic from scenario, seed and engine version. The engine writes result.json, chart.svg, report.html and report.json, a table.csv for the kinds that define one, and on request SP3, CCSDS OMM and OEM, CZML, KML, GeoJSON, STK and SigMF exports; a suite writes study.json and study.html. Those files feed Kshana Studio in the browser, an AI assistant through the kshana-mcp server, and continuous integration.
seg-76e3ec458441 = Så flödar en körning. En scenario-TOML-fil (en typ, ett frö och dess parametrar) går in i motorn, kshana { $version }, genom api::run_toml-anropet över { "87" } scenariotyper, deterministiskt utifrån scenario, frö och motorversion. Motorn skriver result.json, chart.svg, report.html och report.json, en table.csv för de typer som definierar en, och på begäran exporter i SP3, CCSDS OMM och OEM, CZML, KML, GeoJSON, STK och SigMF; en svit skriver study.json och study.html. Dessa filer matar Kshana Studio i webbläsaren, en AI-assistent via servern kshana-mcp och kontinuerlig integration.

# <b>{ "176" } of { "388" }</b> capabilities validated against independent external oracles; { "208" } honestly labelled Modelled. Each row of the verification matrix names a capability, the oracle it is checked against, the test that runs the check, and the label that follows. Continuous integration (CI) makes it impossible to call a capability VALIDATED without an independent external oracle.
seg-707e2cf795f6 = <b>{ "176" } av { "388" }</b> förmågor validerade mot oberoende externa referenser; { "208" } ärligt märkta Modelled. Varje rad i verifieringsmatrisen anger en förmåga, den oberoende referens den kontrolleras mot, testet som utför kontrollen och märkningen som följer. Kontinuerlig integration (CI) gör det omöjligt att kalla en förmåga VALIDATED utan en oberoende extern referens.

# How a capability earns its label. Each row of the verification matrix names a capability, the oracle it is checked against, the test that runs the check in continuous integration, and the label that follows. A row may be VALIDATED only with an independent external oracle; otherwise it is MODELLED, or PARTNER when a hardware partner owns it. Example: SGP4/SDP4, Cowell 6-DOF + perturbations, batch/sequential OD, oracle AIAA 2006-6753 SGP4 verification vectors, test tests/sgp4_verification.rs, label VALIDATED. Live counts: { "176" } VALIDATED, { "208" } MODELLED, { "4" } PARTNER of { "388" } rows. In all, { "176" } capabilities validated against independent external oracles; { "208" } more are honestly labelled MODELLED and { "4" } are PARTNER-owned.
seg-2f81cf391eae = Så förtjänar en förmåga sin märkning. Varje rad i verifieringsmatrisen anger en förmåga, den oberoende referens den kontrolleras mot, testet som utför kontrollen i kontinuerlig integration och märkningen som följer. En rad får vara VALIDATED endast med en oberoende extern referens; annars är den MODELLED, eller PARTNER när en maskinvarupartner äger den. Exempel: SGP4/SDP4, Cowell 6-DOF + perturbationer, batch-/sekventiell OD, referens AIAA 2006-6753 SGP4-verifieringsvektorer, test tests/sgp4_verification.rs, märkning VALIDATED. Aktuellt antal: { "176" } VALIDATED, { "208" } MODELLED, { "4" } PARTNER av { "388" } rader. Sammanlagt är { "176" } förmågor validerade mot oberoende externa referenser; { "208" } till är ärligt märkta MODELLED och { "4" } ägs av en PARTNER.

# Kshana's architecture: one open engine at the centre, kshana { $version } under the AGPL-3.0, with api::run_toml, a typed dispatch over every kind, and the verification ledger of { "388" } capabilities ({ "176" } VALIDATED, { "208" } MODELLED, { "4" } PARTNER). Around it, every surface runs the same engine: Command line (cargo install kshana); Rust library (cargo add kshana); Python (pip install kshana); WebAssembly + Kshana Studio (npm install kshana); MCP server (cargo install kshana-mcp); Docker image (ghcr.io/ashfordeou/kshana-mcp); JetBrains plugin (Marketplace: "Kshana"). Below it, Kshana Pro, a proprietary overlay that depends on the open engine as a library and never forks it, and adds no physical model.
seg-9d69cf43cd40 = Kshanas arkitektur: en öppen motor i mitten, kshana { $version } under AGPL-3.0, med api::run_toml, en typad dispatch över varje typ, och verifieringsregistret med { "388" } förmågor ({ "176" } VALIDATED, { "208" } MODELLED, { "4" } PARTNER). Runt den kör varje yta samma motor: kommandorad (cargo install kshana); Rust-bibliotek (cargo add kshana); Python (pip install kshana); WebAssembly + Kshana Studio (npm install kshana); MCP-server (cargo install kshana-mcp); Docker-avbild (ghcr.io/ashfordeou/kshana-mcp); JetBrains-insticksprogram (Marketplace: "Kshana"). Under den ligger Kshana Pro, ett proprietärt överlägg som är beroende av den öppna motorn som bibliotek, aldrig förgrenar den och inte tillför någon fysikalisk modell.

# Kshana's architecture in layers. Five front doors (command line, Python, JavaScript and WebAssembly, the MCP server and the JetBrains plugin) reach one api::run_toml, a typed dispatch over { "87" } kinds. Beneath it sit eight domain layers: time and frames, clocks and timing, inertial and fusion, GNSS and integrity, astrodynamics, LEO PNT, Moon, Mars and deep space, and missions and studies, on a shared core, all cross-referenced by the verification module, the machine-checked matrix of { "388" } capabilities that is the single source of truth for every label.
seg-9bc9a971bdb4 = Kshanas arkitektur i skikt. Fem ingångar (kommandorad, Python, JavaScript och WebAssembly, MCP-servern och JetBrains-insticksprogrammet) når en enda api::run_toml, en typad dispatch över { "87" } typer. Under den ligger åtta domänskikt: tid och referenssystem, klockor och tidssynkronisering, tröghet och fusion, GNSS och integritet, astrodynamik, LEO PNT, månen, Mars och djupa rymden samt uppdrag och studier, på en gemensam kärna, alla korsrefererade av verifieringsmodulen, den maskingranskade matrisen med { "388" } förmågor som är den enda sanningskällan för varje märkning.

# Closed-form identities and internal consistency; NO external oracle is claimed. The limb half angle is checked against asin(R_earth/r_orbit) and the geometry against it; the free-space loss, the DOP kernel and the SGP4 propagation this composes are each externally validated in their own rows and this row does not borrow their status. WHY MODELLED, and it is the transmit pattern: the gain at angle is a uniformly illuminated circular aperture, the Airy pattern, while a real GPS L1 antenna is a twelve-element helical array with a shaped main lobe peaked off-boresight to even out power across the Earth disc and sidelobes that are not Airy. Published measured patterns exist and are not vendored here, so orderings and orders of magnitude carry, while the dB of any single satellite does not. A FIRST IMPLEMENTATION OF THIS ROW WAS WRONG IN A WAY WORTH RECORDING: the Airy expression is valid only in the forward hemisphere, its argument goes as sin(theta), and at theta near { "180" } deg it wraps around and returns FULL BORESIGHT GAIN behind the aperture - so the two strongest links in the first report were satellites pointing their antennas away from the Moon, and they were the only two that cleared the threshold. Behind the aperture the model now applies a flat back-lobe floor, stated as a bound rather than a fabricated pattern shape. Deliberately absent, each making the budget OPTIMISTIC: no ionospheric or tropospheric loss on the limb-grazing rays, no polarisation, pointing or implementation loss, and a spherical Earth with no refractive extension. The Moon position is an INPUT, not an ephemeris lookup, because the quantity under test is the link and the beam geometry. Upgrading this row to Validated needs a measured transmit pattern and LuGRE normal points to check against; neither is in the repository and neither is invented here. { "0.30" } round { "2" }, a finding (stays MODELLED): a measured-input link path (real SP3 positions, per-block transmit power, tabulated transmit and receive patterns, acquisition and tracking hysteresis) was checked against Montenbruck et al. { "2023" } Table { "3" } for GENESIS at { "6000" } km (pre-registered 5b414f0a): zenith-antenna means agree on { "5" } of { "6" } within one dB of the paper sensitivity; nadir-antenna means are { "1.0" } to { "2.4" } satellites higher than printed, plausibly because the paper used azimuth-dependent GPS side-lobe patterns that are not published. The lunar-distance figures of this row still use the Airy stand-in. { "0.30" }+ D7, a finding (stays MODELLED): the restated quantity, the C/N0 DIFFERENCE between Block IIR/IIR-M satellites tracked at one epoch, predicted with the NAVCEN per-SVN measured L1 patterns (two-dimensional, yaw-steering frame) against the LuGRE flight C/N0 (pre-registered 768cb62b: { "99" } % visible, pair RMS <= { "3.0" } dB, median <= { "2.0" } dB, at least { "50" } records and { "30" } pairs) has too little data: the { "26" } qualifying transit and lunar-orbit epochs hold { "15" } tracked IIR/IIR-M records (mostly IIF and III are tracked) and one pair. All { "15" } are predicted visible and the single pair's residual is -0.06 dB; nothing is claimed

EN FÖRSTA IMPLEMENTATION AV DEN HÄR RADEN VAR FEL PÅ ETT SÄTT SOM ÄR VÄRT ATT NOTERA: Airy-uttrycket gäller bara i framåthalvklotet, dess argument går som sin(theta), och vid theta nära { "180" } deg viker det runt och ger FULL FÖRSTÄRKNING I SIKTLINJEN bakom aperturen - så de två starkaste länkarna i den första rapporten var satelliter som riktade sina antenner bort från Månen, och de var de enda två som klarade tröskeln. # Two internal oracles, no external dataset. ({ "1" }) Closed-form round trip: the minimum detectable bias is produced by inverting the non-central chi-square tail on the non-centrality (raim::pbias) and is verified by feeding the resulting non-centrality back through raim::noncentral_chi2_cdf at the monitor's own threshold, which must return P_md. ({ "2" }) Injection vs analysis: a bias is written into the RF estimate of one axis and cross_raim::run_cross_raim is re-run, so the statistic compared against the analytic non-centrality is the one the monitor computed, not a re-derivation. A seeded Monte-Carlo of the statistic itself is carried in the tests as a third check; it is deliberately NOT in the report, because a sampled estimate would be slower and not reproducible bit-for-bit. ExternalDataset is declined: the quantity is the detection power of THIS monitor at THIS scenario's sigma allocation, and the sigma magnitudes the MDB is scaled by are Modelled representative inputs, not measurements. ReferenceImpl was also considered and declined — the Monte-Carlo samples the same statistic the analysis describes, so it catches transcription and coefficient errors but is not an independent implementation of the monitor. { "0.30" } round { "2" } (pre-registered 0135a9e4), blocked (stays MODELLED): SciPy { "1.18.1" } chi2/ncx2/brentq reproduce the report threshold (within { "5.4e-12" }), per-axis MDB, power curve, crossing multiple and ramp time-to-detect (within { "5.3e-10" }) on five configurations; the injected-fault statistics have no independent oracle and the sigma magnitudes are Modelled inputs, so the scoping awaits an owner decision
seg-b6b8dec4dcff = Två interna oberoende referenser, ingen extern datamängd. ({ "1" }) Rundtur med slutet uttryck: den minsta detekterbara biasen produceras genom att invertera den icke-centrala chi-tvåsvansen på icke-centraliteten (raim::pbias) och verifieras genom att mata tillbaka den resulterande icke-centraliteten genom raim::noncentral_chi2_cdf vid övervakarens egen tröskel, som måste returnera P_md. ({ "2" }) Injektion mot analys: en bias skrivs in i RF-skattningen för en axel och cross_raim::run_cross_raim körs om, så den statistika som jämförs med den analytiska icke-centraliteten är den som övervakaren beräknade och inte en omräkning. En seedad Monte Carlo av själva statistikan finns med i testerna som en tredje kontroll; den ingår medvetet INTE i rapporten, eftersom en samplad skattning vore långsammare och inte reproducerbar bit för bit. ExternalDataset avböjs: storheten är DENNA övervakares detektionsstyrka vid DETTA scenarios sigmaallokering, och de sigmastorlekar som MDB skalas med är Modelled representativa indata, inte mätningar. ReferenceImpl övervägdes och avböjdes också — Monte Carlo samplar samma statistika som analysen beskriver, så den fångar transkriptions- och koefficientfel men är inte en oberoende implementation av övervakaren. { "0.30" } omgång { "2" } (förhandsregistrerad 0135a9e4), blockerad (förblir MODELLED): SciPy { "1.18.1" } chi2/ncx2/brentq återger rapportens tröskel (inom { "5.4e-12" }), MDB per axel, styrkekurvan, passagemultipeln och tiden till detektion för rampen (inom { "5.3e-10" }) för fem konfigurationer; statistikorna för injicerade fel saknar oberoende referens och sigmastorlekarna är Modelled indata, så avgränsningen väntar på ett beslut från ägaren

# An independent in-repo route. Every Jacobian row is re-derived by central finite difference of lunar_vlbi::vlbi_delay_s evaluated from the state's own Earth-fixed and Moon-body-fixed coordinates rotated forward through the frame chain — a path that computes no derivative and shares no expression with the analytic partials — and the information matrices the two routes build agree to < { "1e-6" } of the largest diagonal. The linear-algebra kernel this composes (information_matrix, crlb, sym_eig) is separately externally anchored against numpy.linalg.eigh / numpy.linalg.inv in tests/fim_observability_reference.rs, and the delay observable carries lunar_vlbi's own delta-DOR far-field oracle; the row does not borrow either status. Closed-form identities checked here and labelled as such: σ² and 1/N scaling, and √(p/trace(M)) as an AM-HM lower bound the computed sigma cannot beat, which coincides with the equipartition value only on an isotropic geometry — which is why the measured ratio is exactly the anisotropy the assumption discarded. { "0.30" } external comparison (Library: NAIF SPICE CSPICE N0067 via spiceypy { "8.2.0" }, MIT; DE440, ITRF93 earth_latest_high_prec, MOON_PA), pre-registered ({ "50863551" }) together with an engine-side model-error sensitivity run: every Jacobian row is rebuilt by central differences of converged Newtonian light-time differences from SPICE geometry (the light-time equation is a short script over SPICE positions; SPICE carries the geometry), with the schedule rebuilt from SPICE elevations. The schedule, rank, defect, information spectrum, condition number, station sigmas, headline, trace bound, equipartition ratios, free-network null space, line-of-sight sweep and beacon declination agree within the pre-registered { "1" } % / { "0.1" } deg, which sits above the engine known analytic-Moon error ({ "3.2e-3" } on sigmas, measured before the run). Validated as a Cramér-Rao bound for the stated reduced parameter set on the stated illustrative inputs: the Moon-centre ephemeris, clocks, troposphere and Earth-orientation parameters are held fixed in both legs, so it is not a real-campaign accuracy

# Closed-form identities and an independent in-repo route, with no external oracle claimed. The Helmert design — the only new derivative in the module — is re-derived by central finite difference of lunar_frame_realise::apply_helmert, a path sharing no expression with the analytic form. The accumulation it composes is lunar-vlbi-fim's, whose Jacobian is finite-differenced against lunar_vlbi::vlbi_delay_s there, and whose linear-algebra kernel is separately externally anchored against numpy in tests/fim_observability_reference.rs — this row borrows neither status. The physics oracle is structural rather than numerical: a beacon delay partial is the near-field DIFFERENCE of two near-parallel unit vectors, so the worst-determined translation direction MUST be the body-fixed direction to Earth, and the test asserts the computed answer against the separately measured direction. MEASURED: campaign-derived translation sigma { "6.3975" } m against the injected-transform scenario's { "0.3125" } m recovery error ({ "20.5" }× tighter), while rotation and scale run the OTHER way at { "2.32" }× and { "13.5" }× looser — the injected path assumes one isotropic sigma and so spreads its error uniformly across seven parameters, which the delay observable does not. Rank 7/7 but condition { "3.8e5" }, with one direction { "99.57" } % pure translation-toward-Earth and forty times worse than any other. WHAT THIS ROW DOES NOT CLAIM: that the campaign figure is right in absolute terms. It establishes that the figure now DERIVES from a schedule, a geometry and an error model rather than from a planted answer. Stays ReferenceImpl/MODELLED: no published lunar-VLBI campaign-plus-datum-covariance pair exists, the station network and delay sigma are ILLUSTRATIVE inputs (the beacon catalogue is sourced, the campaign is not), observations are treated as independent while a real session's troposphere and clock are correlated between nearby scans, and the ephemeris, clocks, troposphere and Earth-orientation parameters are held FIXED. { "0.30" } external comparison, a finding (stays MODELLED): with every beacon Jacobian row rebuilt from NAIF SPICE light times (DE440, ITRF93, MOON_ME; pre-registered { "50863551" }), the schedule, Helmert rank, datum sigmas (within { "4.7e-3" }), condition, weakest direction ({ "0.07" } deg), libration sweep and arc ordering agree on six scenarios within the pre-registered { "1" } % / { "0.2" } / { "0.05" } deg. NumPy on the committed engine Jacobian agrees to { "3e-14" } where the stations are fixed. With the stations estimated it differs by { "6.8e-6" } against a pre-registered { "1e-9" }, whose condition-number premise was wrong (Helmert condition { "2.1e8" }; independent NumPy routes disagree by { "3e-6" }; a contribution from the engine { "1e-9" } pseudo-inverse fallback is not ruled out), so the row is not promoted on that pre-registration. { "0.36" } re-registration at a condition-scaled bar (pre-registered b887eb05; t = max({ "1e-12" }, { "10" } kappa { "2" }^-53) from the conditioning of the joint information and its Helmert reduction), a finding (stays MODELLED, NOT MET): step { "0" } rules the pseudo-inverse fallback OUT for the { "6.8e-6" } gap (no matrix upstream of the Helmert matrix falls back on the six original scenarios, tightest margin a factor { "2.6" }), the SPICE leg still holds on all six, and NumPy agrees within the bar on six of eight scenarios (default, the two arcs, the 3x delay sigma, the collinear defect and the fresh stations-fixed run on 2026-12-01; worst { "0.13" } of its bar). It fails on stations_estimated (datum sigmas up to { "6.8e-6" }, condition { "9.1e-6" }, independence-discarded norm { "3.5e-6" }, against t = { "5.9e-7" }), and on the fresh date the south-pole beacon has no observation, so the beacon information is rank-deficient (pseudo-inverse fallback upstream of the Helmert matrix in both fresh scenarios) and the unchanged NumPy oracle cannot evaluate stations_estimated_fresh (singular matrix). Results: docs/design/evidence/mr05-lunar-datum-cond/RESULTS.md

The comparison bar was registered in commits { "5172e579" } (pycef) and 0a7598aa (Logstash) before either parser was run, and both met it at the first comparison. Five amendments are disclosed. Amendments ({ "1" }) to ({ "3" }) touched no comparison rule and no expected value: ({ "1" }) a corpus-size assertion was corrected from { "5" } to { "4" } band changes, a miscount, since the stream has { "4" }; ({ "2" }) the Logstash runner's pipeline delimiter wiring was fixed after a first attempt that produced no events; ({ "3" }) the trust score and epoch offset were moved from the CEF integer fields cn1 and cn2 to the floating-point fields cfp1 and cfp2, a wire-format change made in this release, after which the corpus was regenerated and both oracles were re-run with the bar unchanged and met it again. Amendment ({ "5" }), { "0.36" }, appended { "6" } geometry-label cases (the corpus to { "176" } lines), registered in 9a8e4fa9 before either parser ran on them: from inputs that carry the geometry label, cs6 gains a third field (';' and the label) and cn1 (geometryUsedSatellites) the satellites used; the Logstash codec's mapping table was read first (no flexString fields, hence cs6 rather than flexString1); both met the bar at the first comparison (pycef { "172" } of { "176" }, Logstash { "176" } of { "176" }), and every earlier reference entry was reproduced unchanged. WHAT IS NOT: neither parser type-checks the cfp values (both keep them as text), so the use of the floating-point fields rests on the CEF dictionary rather than on an oracle; no vendor SIEM was run; the extension key names and their mapping are this crate's choice, and the parsers show only that they are read back as written; the RFC { "5424" } envelope, delivery, timeouts and reconnection are not covered; pycef is a small parser last released in { "2020" }, and the Logstash codec is the more substantial oracle.

# checked against its own closed-form / analytic identity — catches transcription and coefficient errors, but is not an external oracle — Closed-form identities and internal consistency; NO external oracle is claimed. The limb half angle is checked against asin(R_earth/r_orbit) and the geometry against it; the free-space loss, the DOP kernel and the SGP4 propagation this composes are each externally validated in their own rows and this row does not borrow their status. WHY MODELLED, and it is the transmit pattern: the gain at angle is a uniformly illuminated circular aperture, the Airy pattern, while a real GPS L1 antenna is a twelve-element helical array with a shaped main lobe peaked off-boresight to even out power across the Earth disc and sidelobes that are not Airy. Published measured patterns exist and are not vendored here, so orderings and orders of magnitude carry, while the dB of any single satellite does not. A FIRST IMPLEMENTATION OF THIS ROW WAS WRONG IN A WAY WORTH RECORDING: the Airy expression is valid only in the forward hemisphere, its argument goes as sin(theta), and at theta near { "180" } deg it wraps around and returns FULL BORESIGHT GAIN behind the aperture - so the two strongest links in the first report were satellites pointing their antennas away from the Moon, and they were the only two that cleared the threshold. Behind the aperture the model now applies a flat back-lobe floor, stated as a bound rather than a fabricated pattern shape. Deliberately absent, each making the budget OPTIMISTIC: no ionospheric or tropospheric loss on the limb-grazing rays, no polarisation, pointing or implementation loss, and a spherical Earth with no refractive extension. The Moon position is an INPUT, not an ephemeris lookup, because the quantity under test is the link and the beam geometry. Upgrading this row to Validated needs a measured transmit pattern and LuGRE normal points to check against; neither is in the repository and neither is invented here. { "0.30" } round { "2" }, a finding (stays MODELLED): a measured-input link path (real SP3 positions, per-block transmit power, tabulated transmit and receive patterns, acquisition and tracking hysteresis) was checked against Montenbruck et al. { "2023" } Table { "3" } for GENESIS at { "6000" } km (pre-registered 5b414f0a): zenith-antenna means agree on { "5" } of { "6" } within one dB of the paper sensitivity; nadir-antenna means are { "1.0" } to { "2.4" } satellites higher than printed, plausibly because the paper used azimuth-dependent GPS side-lobe patterns that are not published. The lunar-distance figures of this row still use the Airy stand-in. { "0.30" }+ D7, a finding (stays MODELLED): the restated quantity, the C/N0 DIFFERENCE between Block IIR/IIR-M satellites tracked at one epoch, predicted with the NAVCEN per-SVN measured L1 patterns (two-dimensional, yaw-steering frame) against the LuGRE flight C/N0 (pre-registered 768cb62b: { "99" } % visible, pair RMS <= { "3.0" } dB, median <= { "2.0" } dB, at least { "50" } records and { "30" } pairs) has too little data: the { "26" } qualifying transit and lunar-orbit epochs hold { "15" } tracked IIR/IIR-M records (mostly IIF and III are tracked) and one pair. All { "15" } are predicted visible and the single pair's residual is -0.06 dB; nothing is claimed

# checked against its own closed-form / analytic identity — catches transcription and coefficient errors, but is not an external oracle — Two internal oracles, no external dataset. ({ "1" }) Closed-form round trip: the minimum detectable bias is produced by inverting the non-central chi-square tail on the non-centrality (raim::pbias) and is verified by feeding the resulting non-centrality back through raim::noncentral_chi2_cdf at the monitor's own threshold, which must return P_md. ({ "2" }) Injection vs analysis: a bias is written into the RF estimate of one axis and cross_raim::run_cross_raim is re-run, so the statistic compared against the analytic non-centrality is the one the monitor computed, not a re-derivation. A seeded Monte-Carlo of the statistic itself is carried in the tests as a third check; it is deliberately NOT in the report, because a sampled estimate would be slower and not reproducible bit-for-bit. ExternalDataset is declined: the quantity is the detection power of THIS monitor at THIS scenario's sigma allocation, and the sigma magnitudes the MDB is scaled by are Modelled representative inputs, not measurements. ReferenceImpl was also considered and declined — the Monte-Carlo samples the same statistic the analysis describes, so it catches transcription and coefficient errors but is not an independent implementation of the monitor. { "0.30" } round { "2" } (pre-registered 0135a9e4), blocked (stays MODELLED): SciPy { "1.18.1" } chi2/ncx2/brentq reproduce the report threshold (within { "5.4e-12" }), per-axis MDB, power curve, crossing multiple and ramp time-to-detect (within { "5.3e-10" }) on five configurations; the injected-fault statistics have no independent oracle and the sigma magnitudes are Modelled inputs, so the scoping awaits an owner decision

({ "1" }) An analytic signal with known coefficients — the only way to exercise the periodic machinery, since no committed series is long enough to admit an annual term. ({ "2" }) The textbook closed-form least-squares solution, different algebra from the matrix solve under test, on real rows. ({ "3" }) The genuine archived Bulletin A prediction rows the real { "2026" } product publishes, compared per lead as an AGREEMENT statistic and explicitly not as an error: { "0.256" } ms at { "1" } day, { "0.695" } ms at { "2" } days, { "1.252" } ms at { "3" } days, drifting to { "3.885" } ms at { "9" } days. So this is Bulletin A's CLASS, close at short lead, not Bulletin A. The predicted-versus-final residuals are real measured quantities over real IERS rows, but their magnitude is checked only against the DIRECTION of the comparison, never against an IERS-published accuracy number — reading a real product is provenance, not an oracle. # checked against a separate implementation in this same codebase — independent of the unit under test, but not externally authoritative — Closed-form identities and an independent in-repo route, with no external oracle claimed. The Helmert design — the only new derivative in the module — is re-derived by central finite difference of lunar_frame_realise::apply_helmert, a path sharing no expression with the analytic form. The accumulation it composes is lunar-vlbi-fim's, whose Jacobian is finite-differenced against lunar_vlbi::vlbi_delay_s there, and whose linear-algebra kernel is separately externally anchored against numpy in tests/fim_observability_reference.rs — this row borrows neither status. The physics oracle is structural rather than numerical: a beacon delay partial is the near-field DIFFERENCE of two near-parallel unit vectors, so the worst-determined translation direction MUST be the body-fixed direction to Earth, and the test asserts the computed answer against the separately measured direction. MEASURED: campaign-derived translation sigma { "6.3975" } m against the injected-transform scenario's { "0.3125" } m recovery error ({ "20.5" }× tighter), while rotation and scale run the OTHER way at { "2.32" }× and { "13.5" }× looser — the injected path assumes one isotropic sigma and so spreads its error uniformly across seven parameters, which the delay observable does not. Rank 7/7 but condition { "3.8e5" }, with one direction { "99.57" } % pure translation-toward-Earth and forty times worse than any other. WHAT THIS ROW DOES NOT CLAIM: that the campaign figure is right in absolute terms. It establishes that the figure now DERIVES from a schedule, a geometry and an error model rather than from a planted answer. Stays ReferenceImpl/MODELLED: no published lunar-VLBI campaign-plus-datum-covariance pair exists, the station network and delay sigma are ILLUSTRATIVE inputs (the beacon catalogue is sourced, the campaign is not), observations are treated as independent while a real session's troposphere and clock are correlated between nearby scans, and the ephemeris, clocks, troposphere and Earth-orientation parameters are held FIXED. { "0.30" } external comparison, a finding (stays MODELLED): with every beacon Jacobian row rebuilt from NAIF SPICE light times (DE440, ITRF93, MOON_ME; pre-registered { "50863551" }), the schedule, Helmert rank, datum sigmas (within { "4.7e-3" }), condition, weakest direction ({ "0.07" } deg), libration sweep and arc ordering agree on six scenarios within the pre-registered { "1" } % / { "0.2" } / { "0.05" } deg. NumPy on the committed engine Jacobian agrees to { "3e-14" } where the stations are fixed. With the stations estimated it differs by { "6.8e-6" } against a pre-registered { "1e-9" }, whose condition-number premise was wrong (Helmert condition { "2.1e8" }; independent NumPy routes disagree by { "3e-6" }; a contribution from the engine { "1e-9" } pseudo-inverse fallback is not ruled out), so the row is not promoted on that pre-registration. { "0.36" } re-registration at a condition-scaled bar (pre-registered b887eb05; t = max({ "1e-12" }, { "10" } kappa { "2" }^-53) from the conditioning of the joint information and its Helmert reduction), a finding (stays MODELLED, NOT MET): step { "0" } rules the pseudo-inverse fallback OUT for the { "6.8e-6" } gap (no matrix upstream of the Helmert matrix falls back on the six original scenarios, tightest margin a factor { "2.6" }), the SPICE leg still holds on all six, and NumPy agrees within the bar on six of eight scenarios (default, the two arcs, the 3x delay sigma, the collinear defect and the fresh stations-fixed run on 2026-12-01; worst { "0.13" } of its bar). It fails on stations_estimated (datum sigmas up to { "6.8e-6" }, condition { "9.1e-6" }, independence-discarded norm { "3.5e-6" }, against t = { "5.9e-7" }), and on the fresh date the south-pole beacon has no observation, so the beacon information is rank-deficient (pseudo-inverse fallback upstream of the Helmert matrix in both fresh scenarios) and the unchanged NumPy oracle cannot evaluate stations_estimated_fresh (singular matrix). Results: docs/design/evidence/mr05-lunar-datum-cond/RESULTS.md

First run: all { "104" } checks pass (worst variance { "3.2" } %, RMS { "1.6" } %, correlation { "0.012" }, mean { "2.2" } standard errors, ellipse { "0.0039" }). <b>Negative controls</b> fail as required: PDOP doubled fails every geometry; errors growing as 1/sin(elevation) against the unweighted Q fail a variance check on every geometry whose lowest satellite is below { "20" }°. <b>How it composes with the row above:</b> that row validates these geometries' DOP against gnss_lib_py; this one adds the step from those factors to metres through the engine's solver. <b>A first, separate check is kept beside it</b> (<c7/>, <c8/>, run before the pre-registration and reported as such): four hand-chosen skies (open, clustered, canyon, half-sky; σ { "1" } m) with chi-square confidence intervals at { "1e-6" } total over { "20" } checks; ratios { "0.987" } to { "1.009" }, and its own negative controls (a { "10" } % DOP error, 1/sin(elevation) errors) fail as required. <b>Why MODELLED:</b> no external dataset; it does not show that a real receiver's error follows the model, and σ_UERE is an input.
seg-fc3443210358 = <c1/>, <c2/>: en Monte Carlo med frö (N = { "20,000" } epoker per geometri, σ = { "1.5" } m, ChaCha8-frö { "36" } + i) av <c3/> med enhetsvikter, löst från den sanna positionen, på de <b>{ "8" } geometrierna i <c4/></b> (de vars DOP är matchad mot gnss_lib_py), mot σ²·Q där Q är kovariansfaktorn för själva kärnan <c5/> (<c6/>, samma aritmetik). <b>Toleranser förhandsregistrerade</b> (incheckning e50c88d2, före den första körningen, enligt designanteckningen för geometrin i { "0.36" }, avsnitt { "5.2" }), var och en som ett antal standardfel: varianser för öst, nord, upp och klocka inom { "5" } %; RMS för H/V/P inom { "2.5" } %; korrelation öst–nord inom { "0.035" }; medelvärden inom { "5" } standardfel; andelen i { "95" } %-ellipsen horisontellt inom { "0.95" } ± { "0.0077" }. # <b>What the observatory does instead.</b> The question the almanac comparison answers is "could the constellation geometry alone explain this low accuracy?". The base method answers a related question without an almanac: a low share must stand out against the same day's background across the region and against the aircraft's own good reports elsewhere. That is a different and weaker substitute: it cannot say that a quiet-geometry hour was degraded where the day's background is also low. An almanac-HDOP scorer is <b>not implemented</b>. It is an open item, to be built from the paper's full text and the public GPS almanac and pre-registered before it is run; until then it is cited, not claimed.
seg-4f8471649a07 = <b>Vad observatoriet gör i stället.</b> Frågan som almanackjämförelsen besvarar är: "kan konstellationens geometri ensam förklara den här låga noggrannheten?". Grundmetoden besvarar en närliggande fråga utan almanacka: en låg andel måste sticka ut mot samma dags bakgrund i regionen och mot flygplanets egna goda rapporter någon annanstans. Det är en annan och svagare ersättning: den kan inte säga att en timme med lugn geometri var försämrad där dagens bakgrund också är låg. En almanack-HDOP-bedömare är <b>inte implementerad</b>. Det är en öppen punkt, som ska byggas utifrån artikelns fulltext och den offentliga GPS-almanackan och förhandsregistreras innan den körs; tills dess citeras den, men hävdas inte.
